=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N toys numbered from 1 to N, and N-1 boxes numbered from 1 to N-1.
Toy i\ (1 \leq i \leq N) has a size of A_i, and box i\ (1 \leq i \leq N-1) has a size of B_i.
Takahashi wants to store all the toys in separate boxes, and he has decided to perform the following steps in order:

- Choose an arbitrary positive integer x and purchase one box of size x.
- Place each of the N toys into one of the N boxes (the N-1 existing boxes plus the newly purchased box).
Here, each toy can only be placed in a box whose size is not less than the toy's size, and no box can contain two or more toys.

He wants to execute step 2 by purchasing a sufficiently large box in step 1, but larger boxes are more expensive, so he wants to purchase the smallest possible box.
Determine whether there exists a value of x such that he can execute step 2, and if it exists, find the minimum such x.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N
B_1 B_2 \dots B_{N-1}

Output

If there exists a value of x such that Takahashi can execute step 2, print the minimum such x. Otherwise, print -1.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4
5 2 3 7
6 2 8

Sample Output 1

3

Consider the case where x=3 (that is, he purchases a box of size 3 in step 1).
If the newly purchased box is called box 4, toys 1,\dots,4 have sizes of 5, 2, 3, and 7, respectively, and boxes 1,\dots,4 have sizes of 6, 2, 8, and 3, respectively.
Thus, toy 1 can be placed in box 1, toy 2 in box 2, toy 3 in box 4, and toy 4 in box 3.
On the other hand, if x \leq 2, it is impossible to place all N toys into separate boxes.
Therefore, the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

4
3 7 2 5
8 1 6

Sample Output 2

-1

No matter what size of box is purchased in step 1, no toy can be placed in box 2, so it is impossible to execute step 2.

Sample Input 3

8
2 28 17 39 57 56 37 32
34 27 73 28 76 61 27

Sample Output 3

37

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    B = data[1+N:1+N+N-1]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()
    pref = [False] * N
    ok = True
    for i in range(N):
        pref[i] = ok
        if i < N - 1 and B[i] < A[i]:
            ok = False
    suff = [False] * N
    suff[N-1] = True
    for i in range(N-2, -1, -1):
        suff[i] = suff[i+1] and (B[i] >= A[i+1])
    for i in range(N):
        if pref[i] and suff[i]:
            print(A[i])
            return
    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's maybe derive interval method from "prefix bad" and "suffix bad" more formally:
For removing i, conditions:
1. For all j < i, B_j >= A_j. This fails if there exists bad prefix index j < i. Let P = smallest j with B_j < A_j (or N-1 if none). Then condition 1 holds iff i <= P.
Wait if P is smallest bad, and i <= P, then no bad j < i because all bad indices >= P >= i. If i > P, then P < i, so bad included. Yes.
2. For all j >= i, B_j >= A_{j+1}. This fails if there exists bad suffix index j >= i. Let Q = largest j with B_j < A_{j+1} (or -1 if none). Then condition 2 holds iff i > Q. Because if i > Q, all bad indices <= Q < i, so none in j>=i; if i <= Q, bad Q >= i included. Yes.
Thus valid iff Q < i <= P. Smallest i = Q+1. If Q+1 <= P, answer A[Q+1]. Nice.

Let's think if we should use interval method in final code to be concise. But maybe array method is more obviously correct to a reader; however final code only, no explanation. Both accepted. The interval method code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    B = data[1+N:]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    p = N - 1
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            p = j
            break

    q = -1
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            q = j

    if q + 1 <= p:
        print(A[q + 1])
    else:
        print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need ensure B = data[1+N:] length N-1. If data includes exactly N + (N-1) +1 ints. Good. If using data[1+N:] no need slice length; okay. But if there are extra spaces no extra ints. Good.

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    B = data[1+N:]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    p = N - 1
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            p = j
            break

    q = -1
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            q = j

    if q + 1 <= p:
        print(A[q + 1])
    else:
        print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This is intuitive: To maximize chance, remove the largest toy; if existing boxes can't fit the remaining N-1 smallest toys, no removal can help because removing any other toy leaves a set of N-1 toys that is elementwise >= the N-1 smallest? Actually if remove a non-largest, the remaining includes largest and excludes some smaller toy, so the sorted remaining list R_j >= A_j for all j? Let's check: For any removal i, R_j >= A_j for all j? If i>j, R_j=A_j; if i<=j, R_j=A_{j+1} >= A_j. Yes. Thus if B_j < A_j for some j, then B_j < R_j for every removal? Wait if i>j, R_j=A_j > B_j; if i<=j, R_j=A_{j+1} >= A_j > B_j. So indeed the j-th smallest remaining toy is always >= A_j, so if B_j < A_j, no removal works. Conversely, if B_j >= A_j for all j, removing largest works. Nice. This is a simpler proof.

Then to find minimum x, we need remove the smallest possible toy i such that remaining toys fit B. Since if no prefix bad, removing largest works. We need find minimal i with suffix condition B_j >= A_{j+1} for all j>=i. The failures are indices j where B_j < A_{j+1}. If we remove a toy at index i, then for all j>=i, the j-th remaining toy is A_{j+1}; so all failure indices must be < i. Thus i must be greater than every failure index. The smallest such i is max_failure + 1 (or 0 if none). This is q+1. Since no prefix bad, this i is valid. Answer A[i]. This is even clearer.

Let's double-check: If no prefix bad, do we also need prefix condition for removal i? For j<i, remaining toy is A_j, and B_j>=A_j by no prefix bad. So yes. Thus valid iff i > all suffix failures. So answer A[last_suffix_failure + 1]. If no suffix failures, i=0. If suffix failures up to N-2, i=N-1. Good.

This means we don't actually need p check? We can compute prefix_bad; if any, -1. Then compute q; answer A[q+1]. But if prefix_bad none, q+1 always valid. Thus final code can be:
import sys
def main():
    data=...
    N=data[0]
    A=data[1:1+N]
    B=data[1+N:]
    A.sort(); B.sort()
    for j in range(N-1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            print(-1); return
    ans = 0
    for j in range(N-1):
        if B[j] < A[j+1]:
            ans = j+1
    print(A[ans])
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Now, let's decide final code. We can use the simplified version:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    B = data[1+N:]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            print(-1)
            return

    ans = 0
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            ans = j + 1

    print(A[ans])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    B = data[1+N:]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    ans = 0
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            print(-1)
            return
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            ans = j + 1

    print(A[ans])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]
    B = data[1 + N:]
    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    ans = 0
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            print(-1)
            return
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            ans = j + 1

    print(A[ans])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]
    B = data[1 + N:]

    A.sort()
    B.sort()

    ans = 0
    for j in range(N - 1):
        if B[j] < A[j]:
            print(-1)
            return
        if B[j] < A[j + 1]:
            ans = j + 1

    print(A[ans])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
